proposition.

We now begin by showing that
f^{\prime}
is a weak equivalence. First of all, we can use the
nil-invariance property of
K^{\prime}
-theory to assume that
X
is reduced. We now divide
the proof into several steps.
In the first step, we consider the case when
X
is a smooth and quasi-projective

k
-scheme. Then
X
is
G
-quasi-projective by

[
81
, Thm. 2.5]
and
[
89
, § 5.7]
.
Furthermore,
X^{i}(\rho)\in{\mathbf{Sm}}_{k}
by Corollary
6.9
.
Using the Milnor exact
sequence (cf. (
5.15
)), it suffices to show that the map of
pro-abelian groups
f^{\prime}\colon\{K^{\prime}_{n}(X^{i}(\rho))\}_{i\geq 1}\to\{K^{\prime}_{n}({\mathcal{U}}_{i,j})\}_{i,j\geq 1}
is an isomorphism for all
n\in{\mathbb{Z}}
.
But this follows from
[
60
, Thm. 4.3]
.

In the second step, we assume that
X
is a
G
-quasi-projective
k
-scheme.
In this case, we can find a
G
-equivariant closed embedding

\iota\colon X\hookrightarrow X^{\prime}
such that
X^{\prime}\in{\operatorname{\mathbf{Sch}}}^{G}_{k}
is smooth and
quasi-projective over
k
. We let
U=X^{\prime}\setminus X
so that
U\in{\operatorname{\mathbf{Sch}}}^{G}_{k}

is also smooth and quasi-projective over
k
.
We let
{\mathcal{U}}^{X^{\prime}}_{i,j}={\left(X^{\prime}\times U_{i}\times U^{\prime}_{j}\right)}/G
and

{\mathcal{U}}^{U}_{i,j}={\left(U\times U_{i}\times U^{\prime}_{j}\right)}/G
so that

{\mathcal{U}}_{i,j}={\mathcal{U}}^{X^{\prime}}_{i,j}\setminus{\mathcal{U}}^{U}_{i,j}
. We now look at the commutative
diagram of homotopy fiber sequences (cf.
[
87
, Thm. 2.7]
)

(6.4)

\textstyle{\mathop{{\rm holim}}_{i}K^{\prime}(X^{i}(\rho))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f^{\prime}_{X}}
\textstyle{\mathop{{\rm holim}}_{i}K^{\prime}(X^{\prime i}(\rho))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f^{\prime}_{X^{\prime}}}
\textstyle{\mathop{{\rm holim}}_{i}K^{\prime}(U^{i}(\rho))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f^{\prime}_{U}}
\textstyle{\mathop{{\rm holim}}_{i,j}K^{\prime}({\mathcal{U}}_{i,j})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathop{{\rm holim}}_{i,j}K^{\prime}({\mathcal{U}}^{X^{\prime}}_{i,j})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathop{{\rm holim}}_{i,j}K^{\prime}({\mathcal{U}}^{U}_{i,j}),}

where
f^{\prime}_{X}=f^{\prime}
and
f^{\prime}_{X^{\prime}}
(resp.
f^{\prime}_{U}
) is the analogous map for
X^{\prime}

(resp.
U
).
The upper left horizontal arrow (which is the limit of push-forward maps)
exists and the left square commutes by

[
60
, Lem. 5.3]
. The right square clearly commutes.
We showed in the previous step that
f^{\prime}_{X^{\prime}}
and
f^{\prime}_{U}
are
weak equivalences. It follows that
f^{\prime}
is a weak equivalence.

In the third step, we assume that
X
is a 0-dimensional algebraic space over
k
.
In this case,
X
must coincide with its scheme locus. In particular, it is
a 0-dimensional
k
-scheme. It is then necessarily regular and quasi-projective over

k
. In particular, it is
G
-quasi-projective and we are back to the second step.

In the final step, we let
X\in{\mathbf{Spc}}^{G}_{k}
be any reduced algebraic space of
positive dimension. We use Lemma
6.2
to find a

G
-invariant dense open
U\subset X
such that
U
is regular and

G
-quasi-projective over
k
. We let
Z=X\setminus U
be the complement with
the reduced algebraic space structure. It is then easy to check that
Z
is

G
-invariant (cf.
[
87
, Lem. 2.5]
).
We let
{\mathcal{U}}^{Z}_{i,j}={\left(Z\times U_{i}\times U^{\prime}_{j}\right)}/G
and

{\mathcal{U}}^{U}_{i,j}={\left(U\times U_{i}\times U^{\prime}_{j}\right)}/G
so that

{\mathcal{U}}^{Z}_{i,j}={\mathcal{U}}_{i,j}\setminus{\mathcal{U}}^{U}_{i,j}
. We now look at the commutative
diagram of homotopy fiber sequences

(6.5)

\textstyle{\mathop{{\rm holim}}_{i}K^{\prime}(Z^{i}(\rho))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f^{\prime}_{Z}}
\textstyle{\mathop{{\rm holim}}_{i}K^{\prime}(X^{i}(\rho))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f^{\prime}_{X}}
\textstyle{\mathop{{\rm holim}}_{i}K^{\prime}(U^{i}(\rho))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f^{\prime}_{U}}
\textstyle{\mathop{{\rm holim}}_{i,j}K^{\prime}({\mathcal{U}}^{Z}_{i,j})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathop{{\rm holim}}_{i,j}K^{\prime}({\mathcal{U}}_{i,j})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathop{{\rm holim}}_{i,j}K^{\prime}({\mathcal{U}}^{U}_{i,j}).}

We have shown previously that the right vertical arrow is a weak equivalence.
The left vertical arrow is a weak equivalence by induction on the dimension of

X
. It follows that the middle vertical arrow is a weak equivalence.

We now show that the map
f
in (
6.3
) is a weak equivalence if

X
satisfies condition (1) or (2) of the proposition. We shall do this by
induction on
\text{\rm dim}(X)
. We let
X^{\prime}=X_{\rm red}
and

{\mathcal{U}}^{X^{\prime}}_{i,j}={\left(X^{\prime}\times U_{i}\times U^{\prime}_{j}\right)}/G
.
We look at the commutative diagram

(6.6)

\textstyle{\mathop{{\rm holim}}_{i}KH(X^{i}(\rho))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{X}}
\textstyle{\mathop{{\rm holim}}_{i}KH(X^{\prime i}(\rho))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{X^{\prime}}}
\textstyle{\mathop{{\rm holim}}_{i}K^{\prime}(X^{\prime i}(\rho))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f^{\prime}_{X^{\prime}}}
\textstyle{\mathop{{\rm holim}}_{i,j}KH({\mathcal{U}}_{i,j})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathop{{\rm holim}}_{i,j}KH({\mathcal{U}}^{X^{\prime}}_{i,j})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathop{{\rm holim}}_{i,j}K^{\prime}({\mathcal{U}}^{X^{\prime}}_{i,j}),}

where
f_{X}=f
and
f_{X^{\prime}}
is the analogous map for
X^{\prime}
.
The horizontal arrows in the left square of this diagram
are the pull-back maps under the
inclusion
X^{\prime}\hookrightarrow X
and the ones in the right square are induced by the natural
transformation from the
KH
-theory to the
K^{\prime}
-theory presheaf in the category of

k
-spaces with flat maps. One consequence of the cdh-descent for algebraic spaces
(cf.
[
50
, Thm. 6.2]
) is that the horizontal
arrows in the left square in (
6.6
) are weak equivalences.

Suppose now that
\text{\rm dim}(X)=0
. Then
X_{\rm red}
is a regular
k
-scheme and
this implies that the horizontal arrows in right square in
(
6.6
) are weak equivalences. Meanwhile,
we showed previously in this case that the right-most vertical arrow is a weak
equivalence. We deduce that all vertical arrows in (
6.6
) are weak
equivalences. We shall assume in the rest of the proof that
\text{\rm dim}(X)\geq 1
.

To prove the proposition in general under its condition (2),
we choose a
G
-equivariant map
p\colon\widetilde{X}\to X^{\prime}
such that

\widetilde{X}\in{\operatorname{\mathbf{Sch}}}^{G}_{k}
and
p
is a resolution of singularities of
X^{\prime}
.
We let
Y=X^{\prime}_{\rm sing}
with the reduced closed subscheme
structure and let
Y^{\prime}=p^{-1}(Y)
. We get a commutative diagram in
{\operatorname{\mathbf{Sch}}}^{G}_{k}
:

(6.7)

\textstyle{Y^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\widetilde{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{X^{\prime}.}

For every
i
and
j
, the diagram (
6.7
) gives rise to
the abstract blow-up squares

(6.8)

\textstyle{Y^{\prime i}(\rho)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\widetilde{X}^{i}(\rho)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{U}}^{Y^{\prime}}_{i,j}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{U}}^{\widetilde{X}}_{i,j}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Y^{i}(\rho)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{X^{\prime i}(\rho)}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{U}}^{Y}_{i,j}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{U}}^{X^{\prime}}_{i,j}.}

of algebraic spaces over
k
.

By
[
50
, Thm. 6.2]
, the above squares give rise to a commutative
diagram of homotopy fiber sequences of spectra

(6.9)

\textstyle{\ \mathop{{\rm holim}}_{i}KH(X^{\prime i}(\rho))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{X^{\prime}}}
\textstyle{\mathop{{\rm holim}}_{i}KH(\widetilde{X}^{i}(\rho))\amalg\mathop{{\rm holim}}_{i}KH(Y^{i}(\rho))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{\widetilde{X}}\amalg f_{Y}}
\textstyle{\mathop{{\rm holim}}_{i}KH(Y^{\prime i}(\rho))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{Y^{\prime}}}
\textstyle{\mathop{{\rm holim}}_{i,j}KH({\mathcal{U}}^{X^{\prime}}_{i,j})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathop{{\rm holim}}_{i,j}KH({\mathcal{U}}^{\widetilde{X}}_{i,j})\amalg\mathop{{\rm holim}}_{i,j}KH({\mathcal{U}}^{Y}_{i,j})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathop{{\rm holim}}_{i,j}KH({\mathcal{U}}^{Y^{\prime}}_{i,j}).}

The middle and the right vertical arrows are weak equivalences by induction
because
\text{\rm dim}(Y)<\text{\rm dim}(X)>\text{\rm dim}(Y^{\prime})
and
\widetilde{X}
is regular. It follows that the
left vertical arrow is a weak equivalence. As the horizontal arrows in the
left square of (
6.6
) are weak equivalences, we deduce
that
f
is a weak equivalence.

To prove the proposition under its condition (1), we first note that
a toric variety over
k
of minimal dimension is regular. In particular, the
claim holds for such a variety using the right square in (
6.6
).
We next note that for an arbitrary toric variety
X
with dense torus
G
,
there exists a toric resolution of singularities
p\colon\widetilde{X}\to X
.
In particular, there exists a commutative diagram identical to
(
6.7
) (with
X^{\prime}
replaced by
X
) in
{\operatorname{\mathbf{Sch}}}^{G}_{k}
in which all
schemes are toric varieties (whose dense tori are
quotients of
G
), all maps are
G
-equivariant and
\widetilde{X}
is regular
(e.g., see
[
20
, § 2]
).
We now repeat the above proof in the characteristic zero case
to conclude that
f
is a weak equivalence.
∎

We can now define the Borel equivariant
K
-theory as follows.

Definition 6.11
.

Let
X\in{\mathbf{Spc}}^{G}_{k}
be as in Proposition
6.10
. We define the
Borel equivariant
K
-theories of
X
for
G
-action by

K^{\prime}(X_{G}):=\mathop{{\rm holim}}_{i}K^{\prime}(X^{i}(\rho));\ \ \mbox{and}\ \ KH(X_{G}):=\mathop{{\rm holim}}_{i}KH(X^{i}(\rho)),

where
\rho=(V_{i},U_{i})_{i\geq 1}
is any admissible gadget for
G
.
It follows from Proposition
6.10
that
K^{\prime}(X_{G})
and
KH(X_{G})
are
well defined objects of
{\mathcal{S}}{\mathcal{H}}
.

We shall use the following formal properties of the equivariant Borel
K
-theory.

Proposition 6.12
.

For
X\in{\mathbf{Spc}}^{G}_{k}
as in Proposition
6.10
, the Borel equivariant

K
-theories satisfy the following properties.

(1)

The assignment
X\mapsto K^{\prime}(X_{G})
is contravariant for lci maps and covariant
for proper maps in
{\mathbf{Spc}}^{G}_{k}
.

(2)

The assignment
X\mapsto KH(X_{G})
is contravariant for all maps and covariant for
proper lci maps in
{\operatorname{\mathbf{Sch}}}^{G}_{k}
.

Proof.

This follows directly by applying
[
60
, Lem. 5.3]

(whose part (1) is applied to the inclusion

f\colon X\times U_{i}\hookrightarrow X\times U_{i+1}
and part (2) is applied to

f\colon X^{i}(\rho)\hookrightarrow X^{i+1}(\rho)
),
[
90
, Prop. 3.18]
and passing to
the limits.
∎

7.
Atiyah-Segal theorem for Borel construction

In this section, we shall prove the Atiyah-Segal completion theorem
which asserts that the Lurie completion of the equivariant
K
-theory coincides with
the Borel equivariant
K
-theory. We begin by constructing the motivic Atiyah-Segal
map. We fix a field
k
and a
k
-group
G
.
Let
I_{G}\subset R(G)
be the (integral) augmentation ideal.

7.1.
Motivic Atiyah-Segal map

We let
\rho=(V_{i},U_{i})_{i\geq 1}
be
an admissible gadget for
G
. If
X
is a
G
-quasi-projective
k
-scheme,
Corollary
6.9
and
[
60
, Lem. 8.1]

(whose proof works for any
G
) together imply that

K^{\prime}_{i}(X^{i}(\rho))\cong K^{\prime}_{i}([{(X\times U_{i})}/G])
is
I_{G}
-nilpotent for every
i
.
As shown in the proof of Corollary
5.9
, this implies that

K^{\prime}(X^{i}(\rho))
is
I_{G}
-complete. Since
I_{G}
is the radical of a finitely generated
ideal by Lemma
2.5
, we get that the completion map

K^{\prime}(X^{i}(\rho))\to K^{\prime}(X^{i}(\rho))^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{I_{G}}
is a weak equivalence.
Combined with nil-invariance and localization sequence,
Lemma
6.2
implies that this weak equivalence holds for any

X\in{\mathbf{Spc}}^{G}_{k}
.

We can repeat the proof of Proposition
6.10
to
conclude that the completion map
KH(X^{i}(\rho))\to KH(X^{i}(\rho))^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{I_{G}}

is a weak equivalence if
X
satisfies one of the conditions of
the proposition. It follows that the canonical maps

\Phi^{i}_{[X/G]}\colon K^{\prime}_{G}(X)\to K^{\prime}_{G}(X\times U_{i})\simeq K^{\prime}(X^{i}(\rho))

and
\vartheta^{i}_{[X/G]}\colon KH_{G}(X)\to KH_{G}(X\times U_{i})\simeq KH(X^{i}(\rho))
,
induced by the
G
-equivariant projection
\phi^{i}\colon X\times U_{i}\to X
,
factor through the completions
\Phi^{i}_{[X/G]}\colon K^{\prime}_{G}(X)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{I_{G}}\to K^{\prime}(X^{i}(\rho))
and
\vartheta^{i}_{[X/G]}\colon KH_{G}(X)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{I_{G}}\to KH(X^{i}(\rho))
.
Passing to the limit, we get the following.

Proposition 7.1
.

Let
X\in{\mathbf{Spc}}^{G}_{k}
be as in Proposition
6.10
. Then the collection

(\phi^{i})_{i\geq 1}
induces
K(BG)
-linear maps

(7.1)

\Phi_{[X/G]}\colon K^{\prime}_{G}(X)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{I_{G}}\to K^{\prime}(X_{G});\ \ \vartheta_{[X/G]}\colon KH_{G}(X)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{I_{G}}\to KH(X_{G}).

The map
\Phi_{[X/G]}
commutes with lci pull-back and proper push-forward maps
in
K^{\prime}
-theory. The map
\vartheta_{[X/G]}
commutes with arbitrary pull-back and
proper lci push-forward maps in
KH
-theory.

Proof.

We only need to explain the commutativity of

\Phi_{[X/G]}
and
\vartheta_{[X/G]}
with the
pull-back and push-forward maps. To prove this, we note that it suffices to prove
the commutativity of
\Phi_{[X/G]}
and
\vartheta_{[X/G]}
before taking the

I_{G}
-adic completions of their left hand sides. Now, the problem
reduces to showing that the maps on equivariant
K^{\prime}
-theory and
KH
-theory
induced by the
flat projections
\phi^{i}\colon X\times U_{i}\to X
have the desired properties
(cf. Proposition
6.12
). But this is standard
(cf.
[
90
, Prop. 3.18]
,
[
60
, Lem. 5.3]
).
∎

7.2.
Equivariant vs. Borel equivariant
K^{\prime}
-theory

We shall now prove the completion theorem relating equivariant and Borel equivariant

K^{\prime}
-theory. This will be done in several steps.
We begin with the following special case.

We let
G={\rm Spec\,}(k[t_{1}^{\pm 1},\ldots,t^{\pm 1}_{r}])
be a split
torus of rank
r
so that
R(G)={\mathbb{Z}}[t^{\pm 1}_{1},\ldots,t^{\pm 1}_{r}]
and

I_{G}=(t_{1}-1,\ldots,t_{r}-1)
. We let
G_{j}={\rm Spec\,}(k[t_{j}^{\pm 1}])
for

1\leq j\leq r
. For
\chi\in\widehat{G}
, we let

L_{\chi}
denote the one-dimensional
representation of
G
on which it acts via the character
\chi
. We fix
a
{\mathbb{Z}}
-basis
\{\chi_{1},\cdots,\chi_{r}\}
of
\widehat{G}
, where

\chi_{i}
is considered as a basis element of
\widehat{G_{i}}
. Given

i\geq 1
, set
V_{i}=\stackrel{{\scriptstyle r}}{{\underset{j=1}{\prod}}}L^{\oplus i}_{\chi_{j}}
and
U_{i}=\stackrel{{\scriptstyle r}}{{\underset{j=1}{\prod}}}(L^{\oplus i}_{\chi_{j}}\setminus\{0\})
.
Then
G
acts on
V_{i}
by
(t_{1},\cdots,t_{r})(x_{1},\cdots,x_{r})=\left(\chi_{1}(t_{1})(x_{1}),\cdots,\chi_{r}(t_{r})(x_{r})\right)
.
It is then easy to see that
\rho=\left(V_{i},U_{i}\right)_{i\geq 1}
is an
admissible gadget for
G
such that
{U_{i}}/G\simeq\left({\mathbb{P}}^{i-1}_{k}\right)^{r}
.
The line bundle
L_{\chi_{j}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle G_{j}}}{{\times}}(L^{\oplus i}_{\chi_{j}}\setminus\{0\})\to{\mathbb{P}}^{i-1}_{k}
is the line bundle

{\mathcal{O}}(1)
for each
1\leq j\leq r
.
We shall use
\rho
to prove the completion theorem in this case.
The proof is based on the following observation.

Lemma 7.2
.

Assume
G=G^{\prime}\times{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
, where
G^{\prime}
is a split torus and
X\in{\mathbf{Spc}}^{G}_{k}
. Let

\iota\colon X\to X\times V
be
the 0-section embedding, where
V
is an
n
-dimensional representation of
G
on
which the split torus
G^{\prime}
acts trivially and
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}={\rm Spec\,}(k[t^{\pm 1}])

acts via
t
. Then the composition

K^{\prime}_{G}(X)\xrightarrow{\iota_{*}}K^{\prime}_{G}(X\times V)\xrightarrow{\iota^{*}}K^{\prime}_{G}(X)
is
homotopic to the map
(1-t^{-1})^{n}\colon K^{\prime}_{G}(X)\to K^{\prime}_{G}(X)
.

Proof.

We consider the commutative diagram

(7.2)

\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota}
\scriptstyle{p}
\textstyle{X\times V\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{q_{2}}
\scriptstyle{q_{1}}
\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p}
\textstyle{{\rm pt}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota^{\prime}}
\textstyle{V\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{q}
\textstyle{\rm pt}

in
{\mathbf{Spc}}^{G}_{k}
in which
p,q
and
q_{i}
’s are projections and
\iota^{\prime}
is
the 0-section inclusion. The two squares are
Cartesian, vertical arrows are flat and
the horizontal arrows are lci morphisms. It follows that

\iota_{*}(1)=\iota_{*}\circ p^{*}(1)=q^{*}_{2}\circ\iota^{\prime}_{*}(1)
. Meanwhile,
one checks directly that
\iota^{\prime}_{*}(1)=q^{*}((1-t^{-1})^{n})

(cf.
[
95
, Thm. 2.1]
). This yields

\iota_{*}(1)=q^{*}_{2}\circ q^{*}((1-t^{-1})^{n})=q^{*}_{1}\circ p^{*}((1-t^{-1})^{n})
.
It follows that
\iota_{*}(1)=(1-t^{-1})^{n}
.

We now compute

\begin{array}[]{lll}\iota^{*}\circ\iota_{*}&\simeq&\iota^{*}\circ\iota_{*}\circ\iota^{*}\circ q^{*}_{1}\\
&{\simeq}^{\dagger}&\iota^{*}\circ(\iota_{*}(1)\cdot q^{*}_{1})\\
&{\simeq}&\iota^{*}(\iota_{*}(1))\cdot(\iota^{*}\circ q^{*}_{1})\\
&\simeq&(1-t^{-1})^{n}\cdot(\iota^{*}\circ q^{*}_{1})\\
&\simeq&(1-t^{-1})^{n}\cdot(q_{1}\circ\iota)^{*}\\
&\simeq&(1-t^{-1})^{n},\\
\end{array}

where
{\simeq}^{\dagger}
is a consequence of the projection formula
(cf.
[
90
, Prop. 3.17]
or
[
56
, Prop. 3.7]
).
∎

Lemma 7.3
.

Let
X\in{\mathbf{Spc}}^{G}_{k}
. Then
\Phi_{[X/G]}
is a weak equivalence when
G
is a split
torus.

Proof.

Following the above notations, we let
V_{m,i}=\stackrel{{\scriptstyle m}}{{\underset{j=1}{\prod}}}L^{\oplus i}_{\chi_{j}}
and
U_{m,i}=\stackrel{{\scriptstyle m}}{{\underset{j=1}{\prod}}}\left(L^{\oplus i}_{\chi_{j}}\setminus\{0\}\right)
for
i\geq 1
and
1\leq m\leq r
.
We let
G
act on
V_{m,i}
via the product of its first
m
factors.
We claim for
1\leq m\leq r
and
i\geq 1
that the projection

\phi^{i}_{m}\colon X\times U_{m,i}\to X
induces a weak equivalence

(7.3)

(\phi^{i}_{m})^{*}\colon\frac{K^{\prime}_{G}(X)}{((t_{1}-1)^{i},\ldots,(t_{m}-1)^{i})K^{\prime}_{G}(X)}\xrightarrow{\simeq}K^{\prime}_{G}(X\times U_{m,i}).

Applying this claim for
m=r
(and using the identification

K^{\prime}_{G}(X\times U_{r,i})\cong K^{\prime}(X^{i}(\rho))
, we
can conclude the proof of the lemma by passing to the limit as

i\to\infty
and using (
5.1
).
It remains therefore to prove the claim.

To that end, we look at the homotopy fiber sequence

(7.4)

K^{\prime}_{G}(X)\xrightarrow{\iota_{*}}K^{\prime}_{G}(X\times V_{1,i})\xrightarrow{(\alpha^{i}_{1})^{*}}K^{\prime}_{G}(X\times U_{1,i}),

where
\alpha^{i}_{m}\colon X\times U_{m,i}\hookrightarrow X\times V_{m,i}
is the open
inclusion.
Using the homotopy invariance, Lemma
7.2
and the equality of ideals

(t_{m}-1)=(1-t^{-1}_{m})\subset R(G)
, we get (
7.3
) when

m=1
. For any
1\leq m<r
, we let
F_{m}=\frac{K^{\prime}_{G}(X)}{((t_{1}-1)^{i},\ldots,(t_{m}-1)^{i})K^{\prime}_{G}(X)}
.

We then get

\begin{array}[]{lll}\frac{K^{\prime}_{G}(X)}{((t_{1}-1)^{i},\ldots,(t_{m}-1)^{i},(t_{m+1}-1)^{i})K^{\prime}_{G}(X)}&\xrightarrow{\simeq}&\frac{F_{m}}{((t_{m+1}-1)^{i})F_{m}}\\
&{\xrightarrow{\simeq}}^{1}&\frac{K^{\prime}_{G}(X\times U_{m,i})}{((t_{m+1}-1)^{i})K^{\prime}_{G}(X\times U_{m,i})}\\
&{\xrightarrow{\simeq}}^{2}&K^{\prime}_{G}(X\times U_{m,i}\times(L^{\oplus i}_{\chi_{m+1}}\setminus\{0\}))\\
&\xrightarrow{\simeq}&K^{\prime}_{G}(X\times U_{m+1,i}),\\
\end{array}

where
{\simeq}^{1}
follows by induction on
m
and

{\simeq}^{2}
follows by applying the
m=1
case to

X\times U_{m,i}\in{\mathbf{Spc}}^{G}_{k}
. This proves (
7.3
)
in general.
∎

To prove that
\Phi_{[X/G]}
is a weak equivalence in the general case, we need
a lemma. Suppose we are given an embedding
G\hookrightarrow G^{\prime}
of
k
-groups. Let

X\in{\mathbf{Spc}}^{G}_{k}
and
X^{\prime}\in{\mathbf{Spc}}^{G^{\prime}}_{k}
. We let
X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle G}}{{\times}}G^{\prime}
denote
the associated Morita
space with
G^{\prime}
-action. This datum gives rise to two objects in
{\mathbf{Spc}}^{G^{\prime}}_{k}
. The
first is the Morita space
(X\times X^{\prime})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle G}}{{\times}}G^{\prime}
associated to the
the diagonal
G
-action on
X\times X^{\prime}
(where
G

acts on
X^{\prime}
via the inclusion
G\subset G^{\prime}
). The second is

(X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle G}}{{\times}}G^{\prime})\times X^{\prime}
with the diagonal
G^{\prime}
-action.

Lemma 7.4
.

There is a
G^{\prime}
-equivariant isomorphism

(7.5)

\theta\colon(X\times X^{\prime})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle G}}{{\times}}G^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\cong}(X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle G}}{{\times}}G^{\prime})\times X^{\prime}

such that the diagram

(7.6)

\textstyle{X\times X^{\prime}\ \ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\delta_{(X\times X^{\prime})}}
\scriptstyle{p}
\textstyle{\ (X\times X^{\prime})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle G}}{{\times}}G^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\theta}
\textstyle{(X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle G}}{{\times}}G^{\prime})\times X^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{q}
\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\delta_{X}}
\textstyle{X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle G}}{{\times}}G^{\prime}}

commutes, where
p
and
q
are projections and
\delta_{(-)}
is defined in
(
6.1
).

Proof.

We let
Y=X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle G}}{{\times}}G^{\prime}
and
Z=X\times X^{\prime}
. We define

\theta
by letting
\theta(\tau_{1}((x,x^{\prime}),g^{\prime}))=(\tau_{2}(x,g^{\prime}),g^{\prime}x^{\prime})
,
where
\tau_{1}\colon Z\times G^{\prime}\to Z\stackrel{{\scriptstyle G}}{{\times}}G^{\prime}
and

\tau_{2}\colon X\times G^{\prime}\to Y
are the quotient maps.
To show that
\theta
is well-defined, we let
(x_{1},x^{\prime}_{1},g^{\prime}_{1})=g(x,x^{\prime},g^{\prime})=(gx,gx^{\prime},g^{\prime}g^{-1})
, where
g\in G
. Then we get

\begin{array}[]{lllll}\theta(\tau_{1}((x_{1},x^{\prime}_{1}),g^{\prime}_{1}))&=&(\tau_{2}(x_{1},g^{\prime}_{1}),g^{\prime}_{1}x^{\prime}_{1})&=&(\tau_{2}(gx,g^{\prime}g^{-1}),g^{\prime}_{1}gx^{\prime})\\
&=&(\tau_{2}(g(x,g^{\prime})),g^{\prime}_{1}gx^{\prime})&=&(\tau_{2}(x,g^{\prime}),g^{\prime}_{1}gx^{\prime})\\
&=&(\tau_{2}(x,g^{\prime}),g^{\prime}x^{\prime})&=&\theta(\tau_{1}((x,x^{\prime}),g^{\prime})).\\
\end{array}

This shows that
\theta
is well-defined.

We next define
\theta^{\prime}\colon Y\times X^{\prime}\to Z\stackrel{{\scriptstyle G}}{{\times}}G^{\prime}

by
\theta^{\prime}(\tau_{2}(x,g^{\prime}),x^{\prime})=\tau_{1}(x,{g^{\prime}}^{-1}x^{\prime},g^{\prime})
. To show that

\theta^{\prime}
is well-defined, we let
(x_{1},g^{\prime}_{1})=g(x,g^{\prime})=(gx,g^{\prime}g^{-1})
where
g\in G
. Then we get

\begin{array}[]{lllll}\theta^{\prime}(\tau_{2}(x_{1},g^{\prime}_{1}),x^{\prime})&=&\theta^{\prime}(\tau_{2}(gx,g^{\prime}g^{-1}),x^{\prime})&=&\tau_{1}((gx,(g^{\prime}g^{-1})^{-1}x^{\prime}),g^{\prime}g^{-1})\\
&=&\tau_{1}(gx,g{g^{\prime}}^{-1}x^{\prime}g^{\prime}g^{-1})&=&\tau_{1}(g((x,g^{\prime-1}x^{\prime}),g^{\prime}))\\
&=&\tau_{1}((x,g^{\prime-1}x^{\prime}),g^{\prime})&=&\theta^{\prime}(\tau_{2}(x,g^{\prime}),x^{\prime}).\end{array}

One easily verifies that
\theta
and
\theta^{\prime}
are
G^{\prime}
-equivariant and are
inverses of each other. The commutativity of (
7.6
) is also
clear.
∎

We now prove our Atiyah-Segal completion theorem for Borel construction
(note:
\Phi_{[X/G]}
in the theorem below is denoted by
\widetilde{p}^{*}
in
Theorem
1.4
).

Theorem 7.5
.

For any
X\in{\mathbf{Spc}}^{G}_{k}
, the map of spectra

\Phi_{[X/G]}\colon K^{\prime}_{G}(X)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{I_{G}}\to K^{\prime}(X_{G})

is a weak equivalence.

Proof.

We divide the proof into two steps.
We first assume that
G
is a split reductive group which is also special.
We choose a Borel subgroup
B\subset G
and a split maximal torus

T\subset G
contained in
B
. Using Lemma
6.8
,
we can choose a good admissible gadget
\rho=(V_{i},U_{i})_{i\geq 1}
for
G

which is also an admissible gadget for
B
and
T
. We consider the commutative
diagram of spectra

(7.7)

\textstyle{K^{\prime}_{G}(X)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{I_{G}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Phi_{[X/G]}}
\scriptstyle{\alpha_{X}}
\textstyle{K^{\prime}_{B}(X)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{I_{B}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Phi_{[X/B]}}
\scriptstyle{\beta_{X}}
\textstyle{K^{\prime}_{T}(X)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{I_{T}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Phi_{[X/T]}}
\textstyle{K^{\prime}(X_{G})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha^{\prime}_{X}}
\textstyle{K^{\prime}(X_{B})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\beta^{\prime}_{X}}
\textstyle{K^{\prime}(X_{T}),}

where the horizontal arrows are the canonical change of groups maps.

The ring
R(G)
is Noetherian as
G
is special
(see the proof of Proposition
2.3
). Moreover,

I_{B}=\sqrt{I_{G}R(B)}
and
I_{T}=\sqrt{I_{G}R(T)}
by
[
29
, Cor. 6.1]

(see also the proof of Lemma
2.5
).
We conclude from
[
70
, Rem. 7.3.1.2]
and Proposition
5.3
(5)
that the canonical maps

K^{\prime}_{B}(X)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{I_{G}}\to K^{\prime}_{B}(X)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{I_{B}}
and
K^{\prime}_{T}(X)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{I_{G}}\to K^{\prime}_{T}(X)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{I_{T}}
are weak equivalences. On the other hand, the
map
K^{\prime}_{B}(X)\to K^{\prime}_{T}(X)
is a weak equivalence by
[
89
, Thm. 1.13]

(see its proof). In particular, the map

K^{\prime}_{B}(X)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{I_{G}}\to K^{\prime}_{T}(X)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{I_{G}}

is a weak equivalence. It follows that
\beta_{X}
is a weak equivalence.
Since
K^{\prime}(X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle B}}{{\times}}U_{i})\to K^{\prime}(X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle T}}{{\times}}U_{i})
is a
weak equivalence for each
i
(cf.
[
89
, Thm. 1.3]
),
we get after passing to the limit and using the Milnor exact sequence
(cf. (
5.15
)) that
\beta^{\prime}_{X}
is also a weak equivalence.

We now look at the commutative diagram

(7.8)

\textstyle{K^{\prime}_{G}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi^{*}}
\scriptstyle{p^{*}_{i}}
\textstyle{K^{\prime}_{G}(X\times G/B)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{q^{*}_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\phi_{*}}
\textstyle{K^{\prime}_{G}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p^{*}_{i}}
\textstyle{K^{\prime}_{G}(X\times U_{i})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi^{*}_{i}}
\textstyle{K^{\prime}_{G}((X\times U_{i})\times G/B)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi_{i*}}
\textstyle{K^{\prime}_{G}(X\times U_{i}),}

where
p_{i}\colon X\times U_{i}\to X
is the projection and

q_{i}=p_{i}\times{\operatorname{id}}
. The maps
\phi
and
\phi_{i}
are also projections.
The left square clearly commutes and the right square commutes by

[
56
, Prop. 3.8]
.
Since
\phi\colon X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle B}}{{\times}}G\cong X\times(G/B)\to X

is a
G
-equivariant flag bundle, it follows from
[
89
, Thm. 1.13]

and the projection formula for
\phi
(cf.
[
56
, Prop. 3.7]
or

[
90
, Prop. 3.17]
) that the composite horizontal arrows on the top in
(
7.8
) is homotopic to identity. The same also holds for the
composite bottom horizontal arrow. The middle vertical arrow is same as the
map
p^{*}_{i}\colon K^{\prime}_{B}(X)\to K^{\prime}(X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle B}}{{\times}}U_{i})
by
Lemma
6.1
.

Since (
7.8
) is compatible as
i
varies through positive integers,
we get, after passing to the limit and completions, a commutative diagram

(7.9)

\textstyle{K^{\prime}_{G}(X)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{I_{G}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Phi_{[X/G]}}
\scriptstyle{\phi^{*}}
\textstyle{K^{\prime}_{B}(X)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{I_{G}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Phi_{[X/B]}}
\scriptstyle{\phi_{*}}
\textstyle{K^{\prime}_{G}(X)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{I_{G}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Phi_{[X/G]}}
\textstyle{K^{\prime}(X_{G})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi^{*}}
\textstyle{K^{\prime}(X_{B})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi_{*}}
\textstyle{K^{\prime}(X_{G}),}

in which the composite horizontal arrows are homotopic to identity.
Since
\Phi_{[X/B]}
is a weak equivalence, it follows by looking at the induced
maps on the homotopy groups that
\Phi_{[X/G]}
is a weak equivalence.

To prove the general case, we choose an embedding
G\hookrightarrow G^{\prime}
, where
G^{\prime}
is a
general linear group over
k
and choose a good admissible gadget

\rho=(V_{i},U_{i})_{i\geq 1}
for
G^{\prime}
. Since
G^{\prime}
is special,
\rho
exists
by Lemma
6.8
. Furthermore, this is an admissible gadget for
G
too.
We let
Y=X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle G}}{{\times}}G^{\prime}
be the associated Morita space. We
let
X_{i}=X\times U_{i}
.
We now look at the diagram

(7.10)

\textstyle{K^{\prime}_{G^{\prime}}(Y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\delta^{*}_{X}}
\scriptstyle{q^{*}_{i}}
\textstyle{K^{\prime}_{G}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p^{*}_{i}}
\textstyle{K^{\prime}_{G^{\prime}}(Y\times U_{i})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\theta^{*}}
\textstyle{K^{\prime}_{G^{\prime}}(X_{i}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle G}}{{\times}}G^{\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\delta^{*}_{X_{i}}}
\textstyle{K^{\prime}_{G}(X_{i}),}

where the vertical arrows are induced by the projections.
This diagram is commutative and
\theta^{*}
is a weak equivalence
by Lemma
7.4
. The arrows
\delta^{*}_{X}
and
\delta^{*}_{X_{i}}
are weak
equivalences by Lemma
6.1
.

Using the compatibility of (
7.10
)
as
i
varies through positive integers, we can pass to the limit and completions
to get a commutative diagram

(7.11)

\textstyle{K^{\prime}_{G^{\prime}}(Y)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{I_{G^{\prime}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\delta^{*}_{X}}
\scriptstyle{\Phi_{[Y/{G^{\prime}}]}}
\textstyle{K^{\prime}_{G}(X)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{I_{G^{\prime}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Phi_{[X/G]}}
\textstyle{K^{\prime}(Y_{G^{\prime}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\delta^{*}_{X}\circ\theta^{*}}
\textstyle{K^{\prime}(X_{G}),}

in which the horizontal arrows are weak equivalences.
The left vertical arrow is a weak equivalence because
G^{\prime}
is special.
It follows that the right vertical arrow is also a weak equivalence.
Finally, we use the weak equivalence
K^{\prime}_{G}(X)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{I_{G^{\prime}}}\xrightarrow{\simeq}K^{\prime}_{G}(X)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{I_{G}}
to conclude that
\Phi_{[X/G]}
is a weak equivalence.
This finishes the proof of the theorem.
∎

7.3.
Equivariant vs. Borel Equivariant KH-theory

The following is the completion theorem for equivariant
KH
-theory.

Theorem 7.6
.

Let
X\in{\operatorname{\mathbf{Sch}}}^{G}_{k}
. Assume that
X
is either

(1)

a toric variety over
k
on which
G
acts as the dense torus or

(2)

{\rm char}(k)=0
and
G
acts with nice stabilizers.

Then the map of spectra

\vartheta_{[X/G]}\colon KH_{G}(X)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{I_{G}}\to KH(X_{G})

is a weak equivalence. If
k
contains all roots of unity and
m\in k^{\times}
, then

\vartheta_{[X/G]}\colon(KH_{G}(X,{{\mathbb{Z}}}/m)[\beta^{-1}])^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{I_{G}}\to KH(X_{G},{{\mathbb{Z}}}/m)[\beta^{-1}]

is a weak equivalence.

Proof.

By the cdh descent theorem
[
50
, Thm. 6.2]
, we can assume that
X

is reduced. In particular,
X
is regular if
\text{\rm dim}(X)=0
and our assertion holds
by Theorem
7.5
. In general, we choose a
G
-equivariant resolution
(toric resolution in case (1)) of singularities
p\colon\widetilde{X}\to X

and look at the resolution diagram (
6.7
).

Since the stabilizer of any point of
\widetilde{X}
is a closed subgroup of the
stabilizer of its image in
X
, it follows from Lemma
3.1
that

G
acts on
\widetilde{X}
and
Y^{\prime}
(also on
Y=X_{\rm sing}
) with nice stabilizers.
We now apply the cdh-descent theorem and follow
the proof and notations of Proposition
6.10
(with
X^{\prime}
substituted by
X
).
This yields us a commutative diagram

(7.12)

\textstyle{KH_{G}(X)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{I_{G}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\vartheta_{[X/G]}}
\textstyle{KH_{G}(\widetilde{X})^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{I_{G}}\amalg KH_{G}(Y)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{I_{G}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\vartheta_{[{\widetilde{X}}/G]}\amalg\vartheta_{[Y/G]})}
\textstyle{KH_{G}(Y^{\prime})^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{I_{G}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\vartheta_{[Y^{\prime}/G]}}
\textstyle{KH(X_{G})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{KH(\widetilde{X}_{G})\amalg KH(Y_{G})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{KH(Y^{\prime}_{G}),}

where all horizontal arrows are the pull-back maps.
The two rows are homotopy fiber
sequences by (
6.8
) and the cdh descent. The middle and the right
vertical arrows are weak equivalences either by regularity or by induction on the
dimension. We conclude that
\vartheta_{[X/G]}
is a weak equivalence.
The proof that this map is a weak equivalence for the Bott-inverted
KH
-theory
is identical.
∎

8.
Completion at other maximal ideals

In this section, we shall prove a generalized version of Theorem
7.5

(resp. Theorem
7.6
) which describes the completions of
equivariant
K
-theory (resp.
KH
-theory) with
k
-coefficients
at all maximal ideals of
R_{k}(G)
in
terms of Borel equivariant
K
-theory if
k
is an algebraically closed field
of characteristic zero.
We shall first prove a derived version of the nonabelian completion theorem
of Edidin-Graham
[
31
]
.
We fix an algebraically closed field
k
of characteristic zero throughout this
section.

8.1.
Some technical results

We collect some technical results that we shall use in the proof of the generalized
completion theorem.

Lemma 8.1
.

Let
E
be a connective
E_{\infty}
ring which is an
H_{k}
-algebra and let

R=\pi_{0}(E)
. Let

I\subset R
be a finitely generated ideal,
M
a Noetherian
R
-module and
N

a
k
-vector space. Consider the
R
-module
P=M\otimes_{k}N
. As an
E
-module
via the canonical map
E\to H_{R}
, one has then a natural weak equivalence

(H_{P})^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{I}\simeq H_{(P^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{I})}
, where
P^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{I}
is the
I
-adic
completion
of
P
as an
R
-module.

Proof.

We let
Q=P^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{I}
,
F=(H_{P})^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{I}
and
I=(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})
.
We first observe that the canonical map

\gamma\colon H_{(\pi_{0}(F))}\to\tau_{\leq 0}F
is a weak equivalence since
F
is
connective by Proposition
5.3
(1).
We next look at the maps

(8.1)

F\xrightarrow{\alpha}\tau_{\leq 0}F\xrightarrow{\gamma^{-1}}H_{(\pi_{0}(F))}\xrightarrow{\beta}H_{Q},

where
\alpha
is the truncation map and
\beta
is induced by applying the
Eilenberg-Mac Lane functor to the canonical maps

\pi_{0}(F)=\pi_{0}((H_{P})^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{I})\to(\pi_{0}(H_{P}))^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{I}\cong Q
.
In particular, all maps in (
8.1
) are natural in
E,M

and
N
. We need to show that
\alpha
and
\beta
are weak equivalences.
Assume first that
I=(x)
is a principal ideal.

To prove
\alpha
is a weak
equivalence, it is equivalent to proving that
\pi_{i}(F)=0
for every
i>0
.
To prove the latter claim, we look at the Milnor exact sequences

(8.2)

0\to{\varprojlim}^{1}_{n}\pi_{i+1}({H_{P}}/(x^{n}))\to\pi_{i}(F)\to\varprojlim_{n}\pi_{i}({H_{P}}/(x^{n}))\to 0.

Using the exact sequence

(8.3)

0\to{\pi_{i}(L)}/{(x^{n})}\to\pi_{i}({L}/{(x^{n})})\to~_{x^{n}}(\pi_{i-1}(L))\to 0

for an
E
-module
L
, we get
\pi_{0}({H_{P}}/(x^{n}))={P}/{(x^{n})}
,

\pi_{1}({H_{P}}/{(x^{n})})=~_{x^{n}}P
and
\pi_{i}({H_{P}}/{(x^{n})})=0
for
i\neq 0,1
.
We thus get
\pi_{1}(F)=\varprojlim_{n}~{}_{x^{n}}P
, an exact sequence

(8.4)

0\to{\varprojlim}^{1}_{n}~{}_{x^{n}}P\to\pi_{0}(F)\xrightarrow{\beta}P^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{I}\to 0

and
\pi_{i}(F)=0
for
i\neq 0,1
.
To show that
\alpha
and
\beta
are
weak equivalences, it suffices therefore to show that
the pro-
R
-module
\{_{x^{n}}P\}
(whose all bonding maps are multiplication by
x
)
is zero. Since
{}_{x^{n}}P\cong(_{x^{n}}M)\otimes_{k}N
, it is enough to show that

\{_{x^{n}}M\}
is zero. But this follows at once from our assumption that
M
is a
Noetherian
R
-module so that one can find
n\gg 0
such that

{}_{x^{n+i}}M=~_{x^{n}}M
for all
i\geq 0
.

To prove the general case, we write
I=J+(x)
, where
J
is generated by
at most
n-1
elements and proceed by induction on the number of generators of
I
.
By induction, we get
(H_{P})^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{J}\xrightarrow{\simeq}H_{(P^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{J})}
, and
the Milnor exact sequence tells us that the canonical map

H_{(P^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{J})}\simeq H_{({\varprojlim}_{i}{P}/{J^{i}})}\to{\varprojlim}_{i}H_{({P}/{J^{i}})}

is a weak equivalence. It follows that
(H_{P})^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{J}\simeq{\varprojlim}_{i}H_{({P}/{J^{i}})}
.
We thus get

(8.5)

(H_{P})^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{I}\simeq\left[(H_{P})^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{J}\right]^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{(x)}\simeq\left[{\varprojlim}_{i}H_{({P}/{J^{i}})}\right]^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{(x)}\simeq{\varprojlim}_{j}\frac{{\varprojlim}_{i}H_{({P}/{J^{i}})}}{(x^{j})}\simeq{\varprojlim}_{j}{\varprojlim}_{i}\frac{H_{({P}/{J^{i}})}}{(x^{j})},

where the first weak equivalence is by Proposition
5.3
(6) and the last weak
equivalence is by the fact that the homotopy limit commutes with the
homotopy cofiber.

Combining
8.5
with the Fubini theorem
(cf.
[
17
, Thm. 24.9 and § 31.5]
) and the cofinality theorem
(cf.
[
26
, Thm. 6.12]
and its proof which is valid for spectra) for
homotopy limits, we get

(8.6)

(H_{P})^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{I}\simeq{\varprojlim}_{j}{\varprojlim}_{i}\frac{H_{({P}/{J^{i}})}}{(x^{j})}\simeq{\varprojlim}_{(i,j)\in{\mathbb{N}}\times{\mathbb{N}}}\frac{H_{({P}/{J^{i}})}}{(x^{j})}\simeq{\varprojlim}_{i}\frac{H_{({P}/{J^{i}})}}{(x^{i})}.

Another application of the Milnor sequence now yields an exact sequence

(8.7)

0\to{\varprojlim}^{1}_{i}\pi_{n+1}\left(\frac{H_{({P}/{J^{i}})}}{(x^{i})}\right)\to\pi_{n}(F)\to{\varprojlim}_{i}\pi_{n}\left(\frac{H_{({P}/{J^{i}})}}{(x^{i})}\right)\to 0.

On the other hand, applying (
8.3
) with
L=H_{({P}/{J^{i}})}
gives
us

(8.8)

\pi_{i}\left(\frac{H_{({P}/{J^{i}})}}{(x^{i})}\right)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}{P}/{(J^{i},x^{i})}&\mbox{if $i=0$}\\
~{}_{x^{i}}({P}/{J^{i}})&\mbox{if $i=1$}\\
0&\mbox{otherwise.}\end{array}\right.

Since the pro-
R
-module
\{_{x^{i}}({P}/{J^{j}})\}_{i\geq 1}
is zero for every
j\geq 1

as we showed earlier, it follows at once that the pro-
R
-module

\{_{x^{i}}({P}/{J^{i}})\})\}_{i\geq 1}
is also zero.
Using (
8.7
) and (
8.8
), we get that

\beta
is a weak equivalence. We also get
\pi_{i}(F)=0
for
i\neq 0
.
Using the isomorphism
P^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{I}\cong{\varprojlim}_{i}{P}/{(J^{i},x^{i})}
, we
conclude from (
8.7
) and (
8.8
)
that
(H_{P})^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{I}\simeq H_{(P^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{I})}
. This finishes the proof.
∎

Lemma 8.2
.

Assume that
T
is a split torus over
k
and
I\subset R_{k}(T)
is an ideal
such that
\sqrt{I}={\mathfrak{m}}_{1}\cdots{\mathfrak{m}}_{r}
, where each
{\mathfrak{m}}_{i}
is a maximal ideal
of
R_{k}(T)
. For any
X\in{\mathbf{Spc}}^{T}_{k}
, the canonical map

\tau_{X}\colon K^{\prime}_{T}(X,k)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{I}\to\stackrel{{\scriptstyle r}}{{\underset{i=1}{\coprod}}}K^{\prime}_{T}(X,k)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{i}}

is then a weak equivalence.

Proof.

We can assume that
X
is reduced.
We now apply Thomason’s generic slice theorem to get a regular
T
-invariant affine
open subscheme
U\subset X
and a diagonalizable subgroup
T^{\prime}\subset T
such
that
T^{\prime}
acts trivially and
T^{\prime\prime}=T/{T^{\prime}}
acts freely on
U
so that

W:=U/{T^{\prime\prime}}
is a regular affine scheme and
U\cong W\times T^{\prime\prime}
as an object
of
{\operatorname{\mathbf{Sch}}}^{T}_{k}
. It follows from Lemma
6.1
that

K^{\prime}_{T}(U,k)\simeq K^{\prime}_{T^{\prime}}(W,k)
. Using Noetherian induction and the
localization theorem, it suffices to show that
\tau_{U}
is a weak equivalence.
Since the canonical map
K^{\prime}_{T}(U,k)\to\mathop{{\rm holim}}_{n}\tau_{\leq n}K^{\prime}_{T}(U,k)
is
a weak equivalence and the derived completion commutes with limits, it suffices
to show that the map
\tau_{U,n}\colon(\tau_{\leq n}K^{\prime}_{T}(U,k))^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{I}\to\stackrel{{\scriptstyle r}}{{\underset{i=1}{\coprod}}}(\tau_{\leq n}K^{\prime}_{T}(U,k))^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{i}}
is
a weak equivalence for every
n\geq 0
.

Using the homotopy fiber sequences

(H_{G_{n}})^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{J}\to(\tau_{\leq n}K^{\prime}_{T}(U,k))^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{J}\to(\tau_{\leq n-1}K^{\prime}_{T}(U,k))^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{J},

where
G_{n}=\pi_{n}(K^{\prime}_{T}(U,k))
and
J\subset R_{k}(T)
any ideal, we are finally
reduced to showing that the canonical map

(8.9)

\tau^{n}_{U}\colon(H_{G_{n}})^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{I}\to\stackrel{{\scriptstyle r}}{{\underset{i=1}{\coprod}}}(H_{G_{n}})^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{i}}

is a weak equivalence for every
n\geq 0
.

To show that (
8.9
) is a weak equivalence,
we note that
G_{n}\cong K^{\prime}_{n}(W,k)\otimes_{k}R_{k}(T^{\prime})
by Corollary
4.5

(see also
[
85
, Lem. 5.6]
) and
R_{k}(T^{\prime})
is a Noetherian

R_{k}(T)
-module.
Lemma
8.1
(with
M=R_{k}(T^{\prime})
and
N=K^{\prime}_{n}(W,k)
) therefore says
that
\tau^{n}_{U}
in (
8.9
) is a weak equivalence if and only if
the map
H_{(G_{n})^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{I}}\to\stackrel{{\scriptstyle r}}{{\underset{i=1}{\coprod}}}H_{(G_{n})^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{i}}}
is a weak equivalence. Equivalently,
\tau^{n}_{U}
is a weak
equivalence if and only if the map of
R_{k}(T)
-modules

(G_{n})^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{I}\to\ \stackrel{{\scriptstyle r}}{{\underset{i=1}{\bigoplus}}}(G_{n})^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{i}}

is an isomorphism. But the latter assertion is clear from the definition of the
completion of modules over a commutative ring.
∎

We now fix
G\in{\mathbf{Grp}}_{k}
and
g\in G_{s}(k)
.
We let
Z_{g}
denote the centralizer of
g
in
G
. Let
\Psi\subset G(k)

denote the conjugacy class of
g
. This hypothesis will remain in place
until we reach §
8.3

Lemma 8.3
.

Assume that
g\in Z(G)
and
X\in{\mathbf{Spc}}^{G}_{k}
such that
X^{g}=\emptyset
. Then

K^{\prime}_{G}(X,k)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{g}}
is weakly contractible.

Proof.

This is easily proved by a routine modification of the proof of

[
31
, Thm. 5.1]
. We omit the details.
∎

Corollary 8.4
.

Assume that
g\in Z(G)
and
X\in{\mathbf{Spc}}^{G}_{k}
. Then the push-forward map

\lambda_{X}\colon K^{\prime}_{G}(X^{g},k)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{g}}\to K^{\prime}_{G}(X,k)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{g}}
is a weak
equivalence.

Proof.

Using the localization theorem, this is equivalent to saying that

K^{\prime}_{G}(X\setminus X^{g},k)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{g}}
is weakly contractible. But this follows
from Lemma
8.3
.
∎

8.2.
The derived nonabelian completion theorem

We first consider a special case. We assume that
G

is a connected reductive
k
-group such that
Z_{g}
is also connected reductive
having the same rank as that of
G
. We also assume that
G
and
Z_{g}
have simply
connected commutator subgroups. We choose a common maximal torus
T
of
G
and

Z_{g}
which contains
g
. We let
W
(resp.
W^{\prime}
) be the Weyl group of
G

(resp.
Z_{g}
) with respect to
T
. We then have a canonical inclusion of groups

W^{\prime}={N_{Z_{g}}(T)}/T\hookrightarrow{N_{G}(T)}/T=W
. By
[
31
, Lem. 4.6]
,
we have
\Psi\cap T=\{t_{0}=g,t_{1},\ldots,t_{r-1}\}
on which
W
acts
transitively and the stabilizer subgroup of
g
is
W^{\prime}
via the above inclusion.
We let
X\in{\mathbf{Spc}}^{G}_{k}
.

It is easy to see that the first of the two canonical projection maps of stacks

[X/T]\to[X/{N_{G}(T)}]\to[X/G]
is a
W
-torsor. In particular,
[X/T]
is
a stack with
W
-action. It follows that
K^{\prime}_{T}(X)\simeq K^{\prime}([X/T])
is a spectrum
with
W
-action and the restriction map
\rho^{G}_{T}\colon K^{\prime}_{G}(X)\to K^{\prime}_{T}(X)

is a
W
-equivariant morphism of spectra with respect to the trivial

W
-action on
K^{\prime}_{G}(X)
.
We let
{\rm Nm}^{G}_{T}\colon K^{\prime}_{T}(X)\to K^{\prime}_{T}(X)^{hW}

denote the norm map, where
A^{hH}
denotes the homotopy fixed point
spectrum if
H
is a finite group and
A
is a spectrum with
H
-action
(cf.
[
73
, § I.1]
).

For any field
K
, let
H_{K}
-mod denote the category of
H_{K}
-module spectra.
One knows that the homotopy category of
H_{K}
-mod is canonically equivalent to the
derived category
{\mathcal{D}}_{K}
of
K
-vector spaces
(cf.
[
78
, Thm. 5.16]
). Since the latter category is semisimple,
we get that the homotopy category of
H_{K}
-mod is semisimple.

Lemma 8.5
.

The change of groups map induces a weak equivalence of
K(BG,k)
-module spectra

(8.10)

\rho^{G}_{T}\colon K^{\prime}_{G}(X,k)\xrightarrow{\simeq}{K^{\prime}_{T}(X,k)}^{hW}.

Proof.

By
[
58
, Prop. A6]
,
\rho^{G}_{T}
induces an isomorphism

K^{\prime}_{i}([X/G],k))\otimes_{R_{k}(G)}R_{k}(T)\xrightarrow{\cong}K^{\prime}_{i}([X/T],k)
of

K_{*}(BG,k)
-modules for every
i\in{\mathbb{Z}}
. Using the isomorphism

R_{k}(G)\xrightarrow{\cong}R_{k}(T)^{W}
(cf. (
2.11
))
and
[
58
, Lem. 3.1]
,
we deduce that
\rho^{G}_{T}
induces an isomorphism

(8.11)

K^{\prime}_{i}([X/G],k)\xrightarrow{\cong}(K^{\prime}_{i}([X/T],k))^{W}

for every
i\in{\mathbb{Z}}
, where
A^{H}
denotes the fixed point subgroup of an abelian
group
A
equipped with the action of a finite group
H
.
On the other hand, the homotopy fixed point spectral sequence
(cf.
[
42
, Thm. 17.5.4]
) shows that the canonical map

\pi_{*}({K^{\prime}([X/T],k)}^{hW})\xrightarrow{\cong}(K^{\prime}_{*}([X/T],k))^{W}
is an
isomorphism. We deduce
that the canonical maps
K^{\prime}_{G}(X,k)\leftarrow{K^{\prime}_{G}(X,k)}^{hW}\to{K^{\prime}_{T}(X,k)}^{hW}
are weak equivalences.
∎

We let
{\mathfrak{m}}^{T}_{\Psi}={\mathfrak{m}}_{\Psi}R_{k}(T)\subset R_{k}(T)
.
Since the homotopy fixed point functor is a homotopy limit
(cf.
[
73
, § I.1]
), it commutes with homotopy fibers
(hence with homotopy cofibers) and homotopy limits.
Using this fact and (
5.1
), we deduce from
Proposition
5.3
(5) that (
8.10
) induces a
weak equivalence

(8.12)

\rho^{G}_{T}\colon K^{\prime}_{G}(X,k)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{\Psi}}\xrightarrow{\simeq}{(K^{\prime}_{T}(X,k)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{{\mathfrak{m}}^{T}_{\Psi}})}^{hW}.

A similar argument shows that the map

\rho^{Z_{g}}_{T}\colon K^{\prime}_{Z_{g}}(X,k)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{g}}\xrightarrow{\simeq}{(K^{\prime}_{T}(X,k)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{{\mathfrak{m}}^{T}_{g}})}^{hW^{\prime}}
is also a weak equivalence.

We now look at the commutative diagram of
H_{k}
-module spectra

(8.13)

\textstyle{\pi_{*}\left(K^{\prime}_{G}(X,k)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{\Psi}}\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho^{G}_{T}}
\scriptstyle{\rho^{G}_{Z_{g}}}
\textstyle{{\left({\pi_{*}\left(K^{\prime}_{T}(X,k)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{{\mathfrak{m}}^{T}_{\Psi}}\right)}\right)}^{W}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{{\left(\stackrel{{\scriptstyle r-1}}{{\underset{i=0}{\bigoplus}}}\pi_{*}\left(K^{\prime}_{T}(X,k)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{t_{i}}}\right)\right)}^{W}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p_{0}}
\textstyle{\pi_{*}\left(K^{\prime}_{Z_{g}}(X,k)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{g}}\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho^{Z_{g}}_{T}}
\textstyle{\left(\pi_{*}\left(K^{\prime}_{T}(X,k)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{g}}\right)\right)^{W^{\prime}}}

where
p_{0}
is induced by the projection to the component
corresponding to
i=0
.

The right horizontal arrow on the top level is an isomorphism by
Lemma
8.2
.
The other horizontal arrows are isomorphisms because

\pi_{*}((-)^{hW})\cong(\pi_{*}(-))^{W}
as we observed above. Now we recall that

W
acts transitively on the set
\Psi\cap T
by permuting its elements.
In particular, it acts on the sum
\stackrel{{\scriptstyle r-1}}{{\underset{i=0}{\bigoplus}}}\pi_{*}(K^{\prime}_{T}(X,k)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{t_{i}}})
by permuting its factors. Since the stabilizer of

g
is
W^{\prime}
, it follows from
[
96
, Lem. 3.2]
that

p_{0}
is an isomorphism. We conclude that
\rho^{G}_{Z_{g}}
is also
an isomorphism. Equivalently, the map
\rho^{G}_{Z_{g}}\colon K^{\prime}_{G}(X,k)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{\Psi}}\to K^{\prime}_{Z_{g}}(X,k)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{g}}
is a weak equivalence. We have therefore proven the
following.

Lemma 8.6
.

Assume that
G
is a connected reductive

k
-group such that
Z_{g}
is also connected reductive whose rank is same as
that of
G
. Assume further that
G
and
Z_{g}
have simply connected commutator
subgroups. Then the change of groups map induces a weak equivalence

\rho^{G}_{Z_{g}}\colon K^{\prime}_{G}(X,k)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{\Psi}}\xrightarrow{\simeq}K^{\prime}_{Z_{g}}(X,k)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{g}}.

We now return to the general case.
Let
X\in{\mathbf{Spc}}^{G}_{k}
and let
\Psi^{g}_{X}
denote the composition of the
three maps

(8.14)

K^{\prime}_{G}(X,k)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{\Psi}}\xrightarrow{\alpha_{X}}K^{\prime}_{Z_{g}}(X,k)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{\Psi}}\xrightarrow{\beta_{X}}K^{\prime}_{Z_{g}}(X,k)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{g}}\xrightarrow{(\lambda_{X})^{-1}}K^{\prime}_{Z_{g}}(X^{g},k)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{g}},

where
\alpha_{X}
is the change of groups map,
\beta_{X}
is the completion at the
maximal ideal
{\mathfrak{m}}_{g}\in R_{k}(Z_{g})
and
\lambda_{X}
is the weak equivalence of
Corollary
8.4
.

We shall use the following property of
\Psi^{g}_{X}
.

Lemma 8.7
.

Let
\iota\colon Z\to X
(resp.
j\colon U\to X
) be
a proper (resp. isovariant flat) map in
{\mathbf{Spc}}^{G}_{k}
. Then the diagram

(8.15)

\textstyle{K^{\prime}_{G}(Z,k)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{\Psi}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\Psi^{g}_{Z}}
\textstyle{K^{\prime}_{G}(X,k)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{\Psi}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{j^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\Psi^{g}_{X}}
\textstyle{K^{\prime}_{G}(U,k)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{\Psi}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Psi^{g}_{U}}
\textstyle{K^{\prime}_{Z_{g}}(Z^{g},k)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{g}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{*}}
\textstyle{K^{\prime}_{Z_{g}}(X^{g},k)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{g}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{j^{*}}
\textstyle{K^{\prime}_{Z_{g}}(U^{g},k)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{g}}}

is commutative.

Proof.

Since
\alpha_{X}
is the change of groups map, it clearly commutes with
\iota_{*}

and
j^{*}
. Since
\iota_{*}
and
j^{*}
are
K(BG,k)
-linear, they commute with

\beta_{X}
because the latter is a completion map. Finally,
\lambda_{X}
commutes
with
\iota_{*}
because both are push-forward maps. It commutes with
j^{*}
by

[
56
, Prop. 3.8]
(see also
[
90
, Prop. 3.18]
) using
the isovariance of
j
(so that
U^{g}\cong X^{g}\times_{X}U
for all
g\in G
).
It follows that
(\lambda_{X})^{-1}
commutes with
\iota_{*}
and
j^{*}
.
∎

We now prove the main result of §
8.2
.
An analogue of this for the homotopy groups of

K^{\prime}(G,k)
is the main result of Edidin-Graham
[
31
]
.
Note however that one can not derive the theorem below from that of
op. cit. because the derived completion does not commute with
homotopy groups. This was in fact the main difficulty
Thomason
[
86
]

encountered while extending the topological
Atiyah-Segal completion theorem to equivariant algebraic
K
-theory.

Theorem 8.8
.

For any
X\in{\mathbf{Spc}}^{G}_{k}
, there is a natural weak equivalence of spectra

(8.16)

\Psi^{g}_{X}\colon K^{\prime}_{G}(X,k)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{\Psi}}\xrightarrow{\simeq}K^{\prime}_{Z_{g}}(X^{g},k)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{g}}

which commutes with proper push-forward and isovariant flat pull-back maps in

K^{\prime}
-theory.

Proof.

By Corollary
8.4
, it suffices to show that
\beta_{X}\circ\alpha_{X}

is a weak equivalence to prove the first part of the theorem.

By the nil-invariance of
K^{\prime}
-theory, we can assume that
X
is a reduced space.
We now use Lemma
6.2
to choose a
G
-invariant open
subspace
U\subset X
which is a smooth and
G
-quasi-projective
k
-scheme.
We let
Z=X\setminus U
with the reduced structure. We let

\iota\colon Z\hookrightarrow X
and
j\colon U\hookrightarrow X
be the inclusions and consider
the diagram of homotopy fiber sequences

(8.17)

\textstyle{K^{\prime}_{G}(Z,k)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{\Psi}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\Psi^{g}_{Z}}
\textstyle{K^{\prime}_{G}(X,k)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{\Psi}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{j^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\Psi^{g}_{X}}
\textstyle{K^{\prime}_{G}(U,k)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{\Psi}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Psi^{g}_{U}}
\textstyle{K^{\prime}_{Z_{g}}(Z^{g},k)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{g}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{*}}
\textstyle{K^{\prime}_{Z_{g}}(X^{g},k)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{g}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{j^{*}}
\textstyle{K^{\prime}_{Z_{g}}(U^{g},k)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{g}}.}

This diagram is commutative by Lemma
8.7
.
If we can show that
\Psi^{g}_{U}
is a weak equivalence, it will follow by a
Noetherian induction that
\Psi^{g}_{Z}
is a weak equivalence. In particular,

\Psi^{g}_{X}
is weak equivalence. It remains therefore to prove the theorem
when
X
is smooth and
G
-quasi-projective.

If
G
is either a torus or satisfies the conditions of Lemma
8.6
,
then
\Psi^{g}_{X}
is a weak equivalence by Corollary
8.4
and
Lemma
8.6
. To prove the general case, we first use

[
30
, Prop. 2.8]
to find an embedding
G\hookrightarrow G^{\prime}
such that

G^{\prime}
satisfies the conditions of Lemma
8.6
and

\Psi=\Psi^{\prime}\cap G
, where
\Psi^{\prime}
is the conjugacy class of
g
in
G^{\prime}
.
We let
Y=X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle G}}{{\times}}G^{\prime}
denote the associated Morita scheme and let

\delta\colon Y^{g}\to Y
be the inclusion of the fixed point locus for the

G^{\prime}
-action on
Y
. If we let
Z^{\prime}_{g}
denote the centralizer of
g
in
G^{\prime}
, then
the canonical map
\gamma\colon X^{g}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle Z_{g}}}{{\times}}Z^{\prime}_{g}\to Y^{g}
is a

Z^{\prime}_{g}
-equivariant isomorphism of smooth schemes with
Z^{\prime}_{g}
-action by

[
31
, Lem. 5.6]
.

We now look at the diagram of (smooth) quotient stacks

(8.18)

\textstyle{[{X^{g}}/{Z_{g}}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\scriptstyle{\beta_{1}}
\textstyle{[X/{Z_{g}}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\beta_{2}}
\textstyle{[X/G]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\scriptstyle{\beta_{3}}
\textstyle{[{(X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle G}}{{\times}}G^{\prime})}/G]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{[{(X^{g}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle Z_{g}}}{{\times}}Z^{\prime}_{g})}/{Z_{g}}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{[{(X^{g}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle Z_{g}}}{{\times}}Z^{\prime}_{g})}/{Z^{\prime}_{g}}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha^{\prime}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\gamma}
\textstyle{[{(X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle G}}{{\times}}G^{\prime})}/{Z^{\prime}_{g}}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha^{\prime}_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{[{(X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle G}}{{\times}}G^{\prime})}/{G^{\prime}}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{[{Y^{g}}/{Z^{\prime}_{g}}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{[Y/{Z^{\prime}_{g}}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{[Y/{G^{\prime}}].}

Each of the arrows in this diagram is either a regular closed immersion
or an isomorphism or a quotient map.
To analyze the induced pull-back maps between various
K^{\prime}
-theory spectra
and their completions, we let
\alpha^{*}_{3}\colon K^{\prime}_{Z_{g}}(X^{g},k)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{\Psi^{\prime}}}\to K^{\prime}_{Z_{g}}(X^{g},k)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{g}}
and

\alpha^{\prime*}_{3}\colon K^{\prime}_{Z^{\prime}_{g}}(Y^{g},k)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{\Psi^{\prime}}}\to K^{\prime}_{Z^{\prime}_{g}}(Y^{g},k)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{g}}

denote the maps induced by taking completions at
{\mathfrak{m}}_{g}
.
We let
\phi\colon X^{g}\hookrightarrow X
and
\phi^{\prime}\colon Y^{g}\hookrightarrow Y
be the inclusions.

We first note that, thanks to the universal property of completion,
the composition

\alpha^{*}_{3}\circ\alpha^{*}_{1}
on the
K^{\prime}
-theory spectra is homotopic to the
composition
K^{\prime}_{Z_{g}}(X,k)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{\Psi^{\prime}}}\to K^{\prime}_{Z_{g}}(X,k)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{g}}\to K^{\prime}_{Z_{g}}(X^{g},k)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{g}}
.
Similarly,
\alpha^{\prime*}_{3}\circ\alpha^{\prime*}_{1}
is homotopic to
the composition
K^{\prime}_{Z^{\prime}_{g}}(Y,k)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{\Psi^{\prime}}}\to K^{\prime}_{Z^{\prime}_{g}}(Y,k)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{g}}\to K^{\prime}_{Z^{\prime}_{g}}(Y^{g},k)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{g}}
.
We next note that the canonical maps
K^{\prime}_{G}(X,k)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{\Psi^{\prime}}}\to K^{\prime}_{G}(X,k)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{\Psi}}
and

K^{\prime}_{Z_{g}}(X,k)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{\Psi^{\prime}}}\to K^{\prime}_{Z_{g}}(X,k)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{\Psi}}

are weak equivalences by our choice of the
embedding
G\hookrightarrow G^{\prime}
. Furthermore, the pull-back

\alpha^{*}_{2}
is the same as the change of groups map
\alpha_{X}
and

\alpha^{*}_{3}\circ\alpha^{*}_{1}
is the same as the composition of

\phi^{*}
with the
{\mathfrak{m}}_{g}
-completion map
\beta_{X}
.
Similarly, we have
\beta_{Y}=\alpha^{\prime*}_{2}
and

\phi^{\prime*}=\alpha^{\prime*}_{3}\circ\alpha^{\prime*}_{1}
.

By considering the pull-back maps between the completions of
K^{\prime}
-theory spectra
induced by (
8.18
), we therefore obtain a commutative diagram

(8.19)

\textstyle{K^{\prime}_{G}(X,k)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{\Psi}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha_{X}}
\textstyle{K^{\prime}_{Z_{g}}(X,k)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{\Psi}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\beta_{X}}
\textstyle{K^{\prime}_{Z_{g}}(X,k)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{g}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi^{*}}
\textstyle{K^{\prime}_{Z_{g}}(X^{g},k)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{g}}}
\textstyle{K^{\prime}_{G^{\prime}}(Y,k)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{\Psi^{\prime}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha_{Y}}
\scriptstyle{\beta^{*}_{3}}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{K^{\prime}_{Z^{\prime}_{g}}(Y,k)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{\Psi^{\prime}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\beta_{Y}}
\scriptstyle{\beta^{*}_{2}}
\textstyle{K^{\prime}_{Z^{\prime}_{g}}(Y,k)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{g}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi^{\prime*}}
\scriptstyle{\beta^{*}_{2}}
\textstyle{K^{\prime}_{Z^{\prime}_{g}}(Y^{g},k)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{g}}.\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\beta^{*}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}

It is easily checked that
\beta_{X}\circ\alpha_{X}

(resp.
\beta_{Y}\circ\alpha_{Y}
) coincides with the map

\rho^{G}_{Z_{g}}(X)
(resp.
\rho^{G^{\prime}}_{Z^{\prime}_{g}}(Y)
) in (
8.13
).
In particular,
\beta_{Y}\circ\alpha_{Y}
is a weak equivalence by
Lemma
8.6
. Since
\beta^{*}_{1}
(via the isomorphism
\gamma
)
and
\beta^{*}_{3}
are weak equivalences
by Lemma
6.1
, we conclude from Lemma
8.9
that

\beta_{X}\circ\alpha_{X}
is a weak equivalence. The commutativity of

\Psi^{g}_{X}
with proper push-forward and isovariant flat pull-back is
shown in Lemma
8.7
.
∎

Lemma 8.9
.

If
X\in{\mathbf{Sm}}^{G}_{k}
, then the pull-back map

\phi^{*}\colon K^{\prime}_{Z_{g}}(X,k)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{g}}\to K^{\prime}_{Z_{g}}(X^{g},k)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{g}}
is
a weak equivalence.

Proof.

By Proposition
5.3
(2), the composition

K^{\prime}_{Z_{g}}(X^{g},k)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{g}}\xrightarrow{\phi_{*}}K^{\prime}_{Z_{g}}(X,k)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{g}}\xrightarrow{\phi^{*}}K^{\prime}_{Z_{g}}(X^{g},k)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{g}}

is a morphism of
K([{X^{g}}/{Z_{g}}])_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{g}}
-module spectra.
By
[
95
, Thm. 2.1]
, this composition is multiplication by

\lambda_{-1}(N^{\vee})\in K_{0}([{X^{g}}/{Z_{g}}])
, where
N
is
the equivariant normal bundle of the inclusion
X^{g}\hookrightarrow X
. On the other hand,

\lambda_{-1}(N^{\vee})\in(K_{0}([{X^{g}}/{Z_{g}}])_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{g}})^{\times}
by

[
30
, Thm. 3.3]
.
It follows that
\phi^{*}
will be
a weak equivalence if and only if
\phi_{*}
is. We now apply
Corollary
8.4
to finish the proof.
∎

8.3.
The generalized completion theorem

We continue with the set-up of §
8
(in particular,
k=\overline{k}

and
G\in{\mathbf{Grp}}_{k}
is arbitrary).
Before we prove the generalizations of Theorems
7.5
and

7.6
to all maximal ideals of
R_{k}(G)
,
we need to extend Theorem
8.8
to
KH
-theory.

Lemma 8.10
.

Assume that
G
acts on a
k
-scheme
X
with nice stabilizers and
g\in Z(G)
.
Let
\iota\colon Y\hookrightarrow X
be the inclusion of a
G
-invariant closed subscheme
such that
(X\setminus Y)^{g}=\emptyset
. Then the pull-back map

\iota^{*}\colon KH_{G}(X,k)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{g}}\to KH_{G}(Y,k)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{g}}
is a weak
equivalence.

Proof.

We first observe that if
(X\setminus Y)^{g}=\emptyset
, then the same is also
true for
(X_{\rm red}\setminus Y_{\rm red})^{g}
. On the other hand,
KH
-theory is
nil-invariant by
[
50
, Thm. 6.2]
. We can thus assume that both
X

and
Y
are reduced
k
-schemes such that
Y^{g}=X^{g}
.
We shall now prove the lemma by induction on

\text{\rm dim}(X)
. If
\text{\rm dim}(X)=0
, then
X
and
Y
must be smooth
k
-schemes
and the claim follows from Lemma
8.9
. We now assume

\text{\rm dim}(X)>0
.

We first consider the case when
X\in{\mathbf{Sm}}^{G}_{k}
. We can assume in this case that

X
is
G
-connected, i.e.,
X=GX^{\prime}
, where
X^{\prime}
is a connected component of
X
.
If
Y
contains any connected component of
X
, then it must be the same as
X

in which case the lemma is trivial. Otherwise,

\text{\rm dim}(Y)<\text{\rm dim}(X)
. In particular, the pull-back map
KH_{G}(Y,k)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{g}}\to KH_{G}(Y^{g},k)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{g}}
is a weak equivalence by induction on
\text{\rm dim}(X)
.
We now look at the commutative diagram of pull-back maps

(8.20)

\textstyle{KH_{G}(X,k)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{g}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota^{*}}
\textstyle{KH_{G}(Y,k)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{g}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{KH_{G}(X^{g},k)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{g}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{KH_{G}(Y^{g},k)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{g}}.}

We just showed that the right vertical arrow is a weak equivalence. The left
vertical arrow is a weak equivalence by Lemma
8.9
and the
bottom horizontal arrow is a weak equivalence because
Y^{g}=X^{g}
.
It follows that
\iota^{*}
is a weak equivalence.

We next assume that
X
is singular and let
Z\subset X
be its singular locus
with the reduced closed subscheme structure.
We also assume that
Y\not\subset Z
.
We choose a
G
-equivariant resolution of singularities

p\colon X^{\prime}\to X
and let
W=Z\times_{X}X^{\prime}
. We then get the
G
-equivariant
abstract blow-up squares

(8.21)

\textstyle{W\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{u^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{p^{\prime}}
\textstyle{X^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p}
\textstyle{W^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{v^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{q^{\prime}}
\textstyle{Y^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{q}
\textstyle{Z\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{u}
\textstyle{X}
\textstyle{Z^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{v}
\textstyle{Y,}

where
Z^{\prime}=Z\times_{X}Y,\ W^{\prime}=Z^{\prime}\times_{X}X^{\prime}
and
Y^{\prime}=Y\times_{X}X^{\prime}
.

By
[
50
, Thm. 6.2]
, we get a commutative diagram of homotopy
fiber sequences

(8.22)

\textstyle{KH_{G}(X,k)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{g}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(p^{*},u^{*})}
\scriptstyle{\iota^{*}}
\textstyle{KH_{G}(X^{\prime},k)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{g}}\coprod KH_{G}(Z,k)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{g}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\iota^{\prime*},h^{*})}
\scriptstyle{u^{\prime*}-p^{\prime*}}
\textstyle{KH_{G}(W,k)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{g}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{h^{\prime*}}
\textstyle{KH_{G}(Y,k)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{g}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(q^{*},v^{*})}
\textstyle{KH_{G}(Y^{\prime},k)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{g}}\coprod KH_{G}(Z^{\prime},k)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{g}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{v^{\prime*}-q^{\prime*}}
\textstyle{KH_{G}(W^{\prime},k)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{g}},}

where
\iota^{\prime}\colon Y^{\prime}\hookrightarrow X^{\prime},\ h\colon Z^{\prime}\hookrightarrow Z
and
h^{\prime}\colon W^{\prime}\hookrightarrow W
are
the inclusions.

It is clear from our assumption and (
8.21
) that
(X^{\prime}\setminus Y^{\prime})^{g}=(Z\setminus Z^{\prime})^{g}=(W\setminus W^{\prime})^{g}=\emptyset
.
As
X^{\prime}\in{\mathbf{Sm}}^{G}_{k}
, it follows that the pull-back map

KH_{G}(X^{\prime},k)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{g}}\xrightarrow{\iota^{\prime*}}KH_{G}(Y^{\prime},k)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{g}}
is a
weak equivalence. The maps
h^{*}
and
h^{\prime*}
are weak equivalences by
induction on the dimension. It follows that
\iota^{*}
is also a weak
equivalence.

If
Y\subset Z
, then we must have
(X\setminus Z)^{g}=\emptyset
. In
particular,
(X^{\prime}\setminus W)^{g}=\emptyset
. Since
X^{\prime}\in{\mathbf{Sm}}^{G}_{k}
, it
follows that the map
u^{\prime*}
in the top row of (
8.22
) is a weak
equivalence. But this implies that the map
u^{*}
in

KH_{G}(X,k)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{g}}\xrightarrow{u^{*}}KH_{G}(Z,k)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{g}}\xrightarrow{w^{*}}KH_{G}(Y,k)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{g}}

is a weak equivalence, where
w\colon Y\hookrightarrow Z
is the inclusion.
Since
(Z\setminus Y)^{g}=\emptyset
and
\text{\rm dim}(Z)<\text{\rm dim}(X)
, we see that

w^{*}
is a weak equivalence. We conclude that
\iota^{*}=w^{*}\circ u^{*}
is a
weak equivalence. This finishes the proof.
∎

The following result extends Theorem
8.8
to
KH
-theory.

Corollary 8.11
.

If
X\in{\operatorname{\mathbf{Sch}}}^{G}_{k}
such that
G
acts on
X
with nice
stabilizers, then there is a natural weak equivalence of spectra

(8.23)

\Theta^{g}_{X}\colon KH_{G}(X,k)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{\Psi}}\xrightarrow{\simeq}KH_{Z_{g}}(X^{g},k)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{g}}

which commutes with arbitrary pull-back in
KH
-theory.

Proof.

Using Lemma
8.10
, we only need to show that the composite

KH_{G}(X,k)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{\Psi}}\xrightarrow{\alpha_{X}}KH_{Z_{g}}(X,k)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{\Psi}}\xrightarrow{\beta_{X}}KH_{Z_{g}}(X,k)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{g}}

is a weak equivalence.
When
X\in{\mathbf{Sm}}^{G}_{k}
, this follows from Theorem
8.8
.
In general, we conclude the proof by choosing a
G
-equivariant resolution of
singularities of
X
as in the proof of Lemma
8.10
, using induction on

\text{\rm dim}(X)
and the homotopy fiber sequence of the top row in (
8.22
).
∎

We can now prove the main result §
8
.

Theorem 8.12
.

Let
k
be an algebraically closed field of characteristic zero.
Let
G\in{\mathbf{Grp}}_{k}
and
g\in G_{s}(k)
.
Let
\Psi\subset G(k)
be the conjugacy class of
g
.
For any
X\in{\mathbf{Spc}}^{G}_{k}
, we then have a natural weak equivalence of spectra

(8.24)

\Phi^{g}_{X}\colon K^{\prime}_{G}(X,k)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{\Psi}}\xrightarrow{\simeq}K^{\prime}((X^{g})_{Z_{g}},k),

which commutes with proper push-forward and isovariant flat pull-back maps in

K^{\prime}
-theory.
If
G
acts on
X
with nice stabilizers, then there is a natural
weak equivalence of spectra

(8.25)

\vartheta^{g}_{X}\colon KH_{G}(X,k)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{\Psi}}\xrightarrow{\simeq}KH((X^{g})_{Z_{g}},k),

which commutes with arbitrary pull-back.

Proof.

We define
\Phi^{g}_{X}
to be the composition

(8.26)

K^{\prime}_{G}(X,k)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{\Psi}}\xrightarrow{\Psi^{g}_{X}}K^{\prime}_{Z_{g}}(X^{g},k)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{g}}\xrightarrow{t_{g^{-1}}}K^{\prime}_{Z_{g}}(X^{g},k)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{I_{Z_{g}}}\xrightarrow{\Phi_{[{X^{g}}/{Z_{g}}]}}K^{\prime}((X^{g})_{Z_{g}},k);

and
\vartheta^{g}_{X}
to be the composition

(8.27)

KH_{G}(X,k)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{\Psi}}\xrightarrow{\Theta^{g}_{X}}KH_{Z_{g}}(X^{g},k)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{{\mathfrak{m}}_{g}}\xrightarrow{t_{g^{-1}}}KH_{Z_{g}}(X^{g},k)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{I_{Z_{g}}}\xrightarrow{\vartheta_{[{X^{g}}/{Z_{g}}]}}KH((X^{g})_{Z_{g}},k).

We now apply
Propositions
5.5
,
5.7
,

7.1
,
Theorems
7.5
,
7.6
,
8.8
and
Corollary
8.11
to finish the proof.
∎

9.
Borel
K
-theory via
{\mathbb{A}}^{1}
-homotopy theory

We fix a field
k
, a
k
-group
G
and
X\in{\mathbf{Spc}}^{G}_{k}
. We begin by describing
Thomason’s completion problem which we wish to study next.

9.1.
The simplicial space
X^{\bullet}_{G}

The
G
-action on
X
gives rise to a simplicial
k
-space

(9.1)

X_{G}^{\bullet}:=\left(\cdots\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rightarrow}}{{\underset{\rightarrow}{\rightrightarrows}}}G\times G\times X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rightarrow}}{{\underset{\rightarrow}{\to}}}G\times X\rightrightarrows X\right)

whose face maps
p^{i}_{n}\colon G^{n}\times X\to G^{(n-1)}\times X
(for
n\geq 1
)
are given by

(9.2)

p^{i}_{n}\left(g_{1},\cdots,g_{n},x\right)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\left(g_{2},\cdots,g_{n},g_{1}x\right)&\mbox{if $i=0$}\\
\left(g_{1},\cdots,g_{i-1},g_{i+1}g_{i},g_{i+2},\cdots,g_{n},x\right)&\mbox{if $0<i<n$}\\
\left(g_{1},\cdots,g_{n-1},x\right)&\mbox{if $i=n$}.\end{array}\right.

The degeneracy maps
s^{i}_{n}\colon G^{n}\times X\to G^{(n+1)}\times X
(for
n\geq 0
)
are given by
s^{i}_{n}(g_{0},\cdots,g_{n-1},x)=(g_{0},\ldots g_{i-1},e,g_{i},\ldots,g_{n-1},x)
.
In the sequel, we shall call
X^{\bullet}_{G}

the bar construction
associated to
the
G
-action on
X
. We let
B^{\bullet}_{G}={({\rm pt})}_{G}^{\bullet}
.

One notes that
X^{\bullet}_{G}
is the C̆ech nerve of the quotient map

\pi\colon X\to[X/G]
. In particular, the composition

\pi\circ p^{i_{1}}_{1}\circ\cdots\circ p^{i_{n}}_{n}\colon G^{n}\times X\to[X/G]

does not depend on the choice of
(i_{1},\ldots,i_{n})
for any given
n\geq 1
. We let

\widetilde{\pi}_{n}\colon G^{n}\times X\to[X/G]
denote this composition.
The collection
\{\widetilde{\pi}_{n}\}_{n\geq 0}
defines a
projection map
\widetilde{\pi}\colon X^{\bullet}_{G}\to[X/G]

from the simplicial space
X^{\bullet}_{G}
to the stack
[X/G]
.
This is a morphism between simplicial algebraic stacks over
k
if we consider

[X/G]
as a constant simplicial stack. It is clear that
each
\widetilde{\pi}_{n}
is a smooth map.

Since the face maps of
X^{\bullet}_{G}
are smooth and the degeneracy maps
are regular closed immersions, we get the pull-back maps of cosimplicial spectra

\widetilde{\pi}^{*}\colon K^{\prime}_{G}(X)\simeq K^{\prime}([X/G])\to\{[n]\mapsto K^{\prime}(G^{n}\times X)\}
.
Passing to the homotopy limits, we get the pull-back maps

(9.3)

\widetilde{\pi}^{*}\colon K^{\prime}_{G}(X)\to K^{\prime}(X^{\bullet}_{G}):=\mathop{{\rm holim}}_{n}K^{\prime}(G^{n}{\times}X);\ \ \ \widetilde{\pi}^{*}\colon K^{\prime}_{G}(X,k)\to K^{\prime}(X^{\bullet}_{G},k).

We similarly get the pull-back maps
\widetilde{\pi}^{*}\colon K_{G}(X)\to K(X^{\bullet}_{G})
and

\widetilde{\pi}^{*}\colon KH_{G}(X)\to KH(X^{\bullet}_{G})
.

The map
\widetilde{\pi}^{*}
can also be viewed as follows. Given a quasi-coherent sheaf
{\mathcal{F}}

on
[X/G]
, the pull-back
\widetilde{\pi}^{*}({\mathcal{F}})

defines a quasi-coherent sheaf
{\mathcal{F}}^{\bullet}
on the simplicial space
X^{\bullet}_{G}

(consult
[
87
, § 1.3]
for the definition of quasi-coherent sheaves
on simplicial spaces). In particular,
{\mathcal{F}}_{0}=\pi^{*}({\mathcal{F}})
is simply the sheaf
{\mathcal{F}}
,
with the
G
-action ignored. If
{\mathcal{F}}
is coherent (resp. locally free), so is

{\mathcal{F}}^{\bullet}
. We thus get an exact functor

\widetilde{\pi}^{*}\colon{{\rm Qcoh}}^{G}_{X}\to{{\rm Qcoh}}_{X^{\bullet}_{G}}
which preserves coherent and
locally free sheaves. In particular, it preserves perfect complexes and
pseudo-coherent
complexes. The induced map between the homotopy limits of the

K^{\prime}
-theory spectra is the map
\widetilde{\pi}^{*}
of (
9.3
).

Thomason’s completion problem:

In
[
86
, § 0]
, Thomason predicted that
\widetilde{\pi}^{*}
would induce a
weak equivalence of spectra

(9.4)

\widetilde{\pi}^{*}\colon K^{\prime}_{G}(X)^{\,\,{\widehat{}}}_{I_{G}}\xrightarrow{\simeq}K^{\prime}(X_{G}^{\bullet}).

Thomason showed that if
k
is separably closed and

K^{\prime}_{G}(X)
is replaced by the Bott inverted equivariant
K^{\prime}
-theory with finite
coefficients prime to
{\rm char}(k)
, this problem has a positive solution.
The problem is otherwise currently unsolved.

Our goal in the next few sections is to solve Thomason’s completion problem in
as many cases as possible. In this section, we define motivic Borel spaces,
identify them with
X_{G}^{\bullet}

and use them to represent Borel equivariant
K
-theory of smooth schemes with
group action in Voevodsky’s stable
{\mathbb{A}}^{1}
-homotopy category of smooth schemes over

k
.

We shall use the following
notations throughout our discussion of Thomason’s completion problem.

(1)

{\rm Nis}/k:
The Grothendieck site of smooth schemes over
k
with
the Nisnevich topology.

(2)

{\rm Shv}\left({\rm Nis}/k\right):
The category of sheaves of sets on
{\rm Nis}/k
.

(3)

{\rm Msp}(k):
The category
\Delta^{\rm op}{\rm Shv}\left({\rm Nis}/k\right)
of sheaves of
simplicial sets on
{\rm Nis}/k
(equivalently, the category of simplicial objects
in
{\rm Shv}\left({\rm Nis}/k\right)
).

(4)

{\mathcal{H}}(k):
The unstable
{\mathbb{A}}^{1}
-homotopy category of simplicial sheaves
on
{\rm Nis}/k
, as defined in
[
72
]
.

(5)

{\mathcal{H}}_{\bullet}(k):
The unstable
{\mathbb{A}}^{1}
-homotopy category of pointed
simplicial sheaves on
{\rm Nis}/k
, as defined in
[
72
]
.

(6)

{\mathcal{S}}{\mathcal{H}}(k):
The stable
{\mathbb{A}}^{1}
-homotopy category of pointed
simplicial sheaves on
{\rm Nis}/k
, as defined (for instance) in
[
97
]
.

The objects of
{\rm Msp}(k)
will be called motivic spaces.

9.2.
Universal torsor over
